She had moved the pumpkin while he was admiring a dog. Mara pointed out that the contest judged delivery, not masculine self-reliance. To the extent that the rules allowed a navigator, she intended to navigate. The other competitors cheered. He accepted their verdict with the wounded air of a man abandoned by agriculture.","The gardeners were already preparing the harvest tables. A clove of garlic lay beside a tiny pine cone, which nobody would confuse after smelling both. The broccoli needed washing. A schoolchild brought a weed to Peta and asked whether every unwanted plant was bad. The answer would depend on where it grew and what it did. A label could guide a question without deciding it forever.","Ever since the first plot opened, its volunteers had been prone to doing too much at once. Milo tried to orchestrate a smaller schedule now. They would get cracking on tomorrow's jobs tomorrow. Anytime he forgot that, his grandmother pointed towards the shed with devastating clarity. The first green sprout could wait while its gardener ate lunch.","The garden was largely run by volunteers. Milo wanted to work efficiently enough to leave time for his literary project. His millennial friends offered to help, having experienced enough existential panic over work of their own. A first-time assistant seemed likely to spill a bucket, so Cassian took the heavier end. Milo's grandmother told him not to idealize the task. There would always be another bucket.","Mara stayed by the teaching table. A flower's ovary, the label explained, did not make the plant a miniature person. Its structures had their own functions. An embryo within a seed would need suitable conditions to grow. The children compared a plum and a cherry, then planted a peanut in a pot. Their interest would not be hindered by admitting that a model left things out.","At the greenhouse, a helper tried to poke the blocked drain with a stick. The effort was ineffective. The trained repairer chose to disassemble the cover instead. He had to rein in a more enthusiastic volunteer who wanted to bend the frame. A new weld would require proper work; their need for speed did not make the old metal less fragile.","The repairer watched the water run downward. He would dilute a cleaner according to its label, use it carefully and dispose of the waste properly. That task was far too easy to rush. His evidence-based plan was less exciting than a godlike promise to fix everything instantly. It would be more useful once the crowd had gone.","The Food Standards Authority inspector watched a cooking group wash mushrooms grown in labelled trays. Wild specimens had been kept out of the meal. The garlic smell began to permeate the tent. An everlasting flower arrangement hung from a beam, dried carefully after last summer's display. It was decorative, not another herb to chop. Milo would spell out the difference on the table card.","A wretched branch had dropped bark onto the path. The gardener cleared it in short order, and visitors watched his little trolley in action. Prior to its repair, it had made work much slower. He did not claim an inborn gift for machinery. He had simply learned to ask somebody who knew more.","The kitchen had one bowl of cereal left. A child wished it contained banana instead of plain oats. The cook added fruit, then asked him to return the bowl when finished. His sister offered to poke the last clove into an orange for the decoration. Nobody seemed able to finish any task without discovering another.","A number of young helpers carried plates from the kitchen. They had cereal for children, soup for adults and cakes for everyone. The garden could take advantage of a good harvest without idealizing abundance as permanent. The seed store still needed a reliable inventory. Half a million seeds would not distribute themselves, however grand the number sounded in a speech.","The gardener hoped to insure the greenhouse again after repairs. The old policy would not go by the new estimate automatically. His ironic smile suggested he had learned that painfully. He would spell out the work done, retain receipts and wait for a proper answer. A damaged frame was maladapted to winter; a vague application would not change it.","Peta had brought a teaching tray from the education centre of the national park. Its plant impressions helped explain the fossil record. The gardener's grandmother found the slow evidence of vanished leaves surprisingly moving. She had grown oak saplings for decades and never thought much about their deep history. The account was enlightening in a way no inflated promise had managed.","The rape crisis volunteers packed a small set of information cards away from the food queue. Anyone could take one privately. Mara offered them a quieter table beneath the pine. They accepted, grateful that help did not require explaining themselves to strangers. The evening could be welcoming without being intrusive.","Cassian finally delivered the pumpkin. He had lost the race. Its judge, an eleven-year-old with an alarming sense of fairness, awarded him a second prize for perseverance. The repair team had cleared the drain, temporarily protected the old frame and arranged a proper assessment. Nobody would insure a building into good condition by signing a form. The committee would get cracking on the remaining work next week, after its volunteers had slept.","Mara sat beside Cassian under the pine. A pumpkin lantern waited on their table. Its crooked smile looked remarkably like his expression when he wanted forgiveness. She had no intention of offering it too soon. He had eaten the last garlic bread, leaving her to choose between broccoli and an extremely earnest salad.","Peta came with a sketchbook. A child had drawn the flower's ovary accurately and given it a princess crown. The picture was not a botanical diagram, but its labels were careful. Mara admired the combination. The child's curiosity need not be remolded into a professional manner before it could be useful.","Peta let the children compare a closed bud, an open blossom and a tiny bulb. She did not ask them to memorize every term at once. They could comprehend one distinction and return for another. The experience was enlightening precisely because she did not pretend they had learned botany in ten minutes.","Milo read a page of his literary manuscript. He had revised its most existential paragraph into an account of losing a whole tray of seedlings. His millennial friends laughed with recognition. Ever since he began writing, he had tried to sound wiser than he felt. The evening made him think a little honesty might permeate the next chapter more effectively than another grand claim.","The garden's drain had been ineffective for years. A small change had helped without solving every problem. Its volunteers would not let one success hinder the remaining inspection. Nor would they go by a single dry afternoon as proof. An unrecognized fault might still need attention. Their newfound patience seemed the most valuable part of the repair.","Milo's chapter on social mobility had grown less abstract too. A neighbour had learned skills here before finding paid work. Another had simply found friends. Besides those stories, the book could depict ordinary mistakes. He would not cheat by inventing successes to make the garden sound more important. More often than not, its small results were worth recording accurately.","The half a million seeds would be packed over winter. Their distribution was largely scheduled already. The plot's purpose was not to idealize self-sufficiency or take advantage of unpaid enthusiasm without limits. It was to grow some food, share skills and give people a place to return. That purpose seemed likely to survive one disastrous wheelbarrow race.","Cassian watched a child remove a weed from beside an oak sapling. Its bark was scarcely rough yet. The gardeners had planted thrift along the path and a flowering bush at the gate. Neither needed his advice. He was content to watch their work in action and accept the fact that not every useful thing required his intervention.","The inspector had left a clean report and a list of routine tasks. To the extent that a small garden sold prepared food, it had responsibilities. Those would be written down. Cassian did not wish to orchestrate its whole future, though Mara suspected he could if asked. He had done enough lifting for one day.","The tree trunk beside the bench held a small sign describing the crop beds. Wheat would grow there next year; a plum tree already shaded the gate. Milo had baked peach slices into the last cake and offered one to Mara. Her choice was immediate. A person could enjoy vegetables and still prefer dessert.","The cake was far too sweet for Cassian, who offered the rest anytime she wanted it. She did not know of a more agreeable use of his generosity. An ironic remark formed and disappeared. She would spell out her thanks by eating the last bite instead.","A petal drifted downward from an ornate bowl. The flower had been cut before it looked unhealthy. Milo's grandmother hoped to dry it for next year's table, though she made no promise of everlasting beauty. Mara thought the brief display quite enough.","A wretched little sprout had been brought indoors to recover. A child wanted to bury it as a failure. Peta asked for patience. It might be maladapted to its current position, not doomed in every setting. Its fate would not decide the worth of the child who planted it. The analogy was plain enough that Mara could leave it unspoken.","A pine needle had fallen into Mara's glass. She lifted it out cautiously and asked Milo to dilute the remaining drink with water. He did so efficiently. A long day had left both of them prone to thirst and bad jokes. In short order, Cassian had contributed another, which nobody encouraged.","Beyond the fence, the national park darkened. The fossil record tray had been returned to its protective box. The children carried their potted plants to the waiting bus. The rape crisis service had gathered donations and several offers of practical assistance. Nobody announced those visitors' names. The evening's kindness did not need to become an advertisement.","Milo's grandmother asked them to dispose of the lantern waste properly tomorrow. The pumpkin would have another use in compost, though she would not put its candle there. She had taught three generations to rein in their fantasies long enough to sort rubbish. Cassian obeyed with unusually little resistance. He had acquired respect for her instructions during the race.","Mara watched him fold the prize ribbon. “You may keep it.”","“I intend to frame it.”","“The first-time farmer's defeat?”","“The first-time farmer's excellent choice of navigator.”","He leaned close. She let him kiss her, tasting garlic and the last sweetness of cake. The greenhouse glass caught the final light behind him. For a little while, neither had another task. The quiet felt like a harvest they could carry home. Their shoes rested in damp grass, and neither moved to hurry the evening."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1013,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0246\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div Mortgage","volume":"婚后外传：公爵破产的三个小时","anchor":"chapter-0247","paragraphs":["Cassian lost a tea plantation, a ferry and half his capital before dinner. Mara had spent six pounds buying a board game and considered the return excellent. Rowan, acting as treasurer, announced that his friend's mortgage had become due. The bottom line was ugly.","The game belonged to Tavi, a software engineering graduate who had designed it during a rainy holiday. Each player governed a small island while building businesses there. GDP measured its fictional production; revenue flowed to separate private and public accounts. The difference mattered, though Cassian had originally assumed his wealth would somehow rescue him.","Tavi had spent a year near the Gulf and borrowed some of its routes for the map. He had originally intended a smaller game, then kept adding ideas. Its currency consisted of paper notes, a fiver looking almost as grand as a thousand. He had formally named its public purse the exchequer. Rowan immediately asked whether it could overcharge its citizens. Tavi told him to read the rules.","“It cannot,” Tavi said. “Your real bank account is not connected.”","Mara kissed his cheek. “A level of fairness you may find unfamiliar.”","A ruby marked the current winner. Cassian wanted it. Mara wanted to watch him pretend he did not. Rowan, knowledgeable about the rules, explained that royalty income from a successful film could rescue a weak income statement. But box office receipts were gross revenue, not profit. Expense, a distribution fee and a sales tax would reduce the profit margin. Cassian called the distinction needlessly familiar.","A set of premium counters arrived with yet another expansion Tavi had designed. Mara refused to open it. Dinner might be postponed indefinitely if the islands acquired international diplomacy as well as debt.","Tavi's islands had natural resources and a modest treasury. A levy could fund a harbour, but its toll affected trade. A tariff might protect a new industry and raise costs elsewhere. Subsidy money was finite. The game did not promise that one number described every consequence. Its creator wanted players to argue, and had succeeded beyond hope.","Mara had chosen a bookshop and a small ferry shareholding. Her livelihood in the game looked less glamorous than Cassian's estate. She kept money in a savings account and refused a high-interest gamble. Her stake remained modest. A pension card gave her steady income; a rental room required repairs. She had never been so tender towards a damaged roof.","She would spend time reading before buying. The little shop's name was an intangible asset, and its goodwill had to survive the next expansion. A best-practice card offered a free check. Cassian tried to take aim at her caution. She offered to bet him a biscuit that his unreflecting purchases would cost more. He accepted to the point of forgetting he had only one biscuit left.","Rowan landed on the court square. He needed bail to leave the game's detention cell. Cassian offered a loan. Mara warned that a bad debt would hurt both sides. They negotiated a rate, then recorded the payment. The arrangement was so earnest that Tavi photographed their faces instead of the board.","A banker space offered equity in a real estate venture. Cassian bought it. Its book value was impressive, but the card's next installment entailed a fee he had overlooked. His pocketbook became suddenly empty. “I have been overcharged.”","The estate's next installment would not entail a further interest rate rise if he paid on time. He could issue equity to raise money instead. The resulting dividend would go to a player who helped him. Mara agreed to consider a small allocation, provided he did not call it charity. Her compensation would be specified before he touched the counter. Their earnest negotiation became the evening's second entertainment.","“You have read badly,” Rowan replied.","Mara's next card offered merit pay for skilled workers. She paid it, losing cash now and gaining production later. The rule's computational model was simple and explicit. No one should mistake it for a real forecast. A disciplined player still had to make choices under uncertainty. Cassian looked incapable of believing she would beat him with a bakery and a boat.","She received a cheque for a film royalty and bought a ruby-coloured counter. He called it unhelpful. She called it beautiful. Their domestic competition had begun to propel the game beyond its advertised time. Prior to opening the box, everyone had expected an hour. Now Tavi moved dinner closer to the table.","The game's jackpot remained unclaimed. One could gamble at almost any turn, but Mara preferred a steadier pension. More than anything, she wanted to preserve enough for tuition at her island's college. The rules seemed to encourage different priorities. No one could reinvent the whole system merely because a current strategy failed. A permissive rulebook still had limits.","The next card was a storm. Cassian's island needed flood protection. His insurance had a deductible; the expense exceeded his remaining savings. He looked at his wife with a suddenly tender expression. She shook her head before he could speak.","Cassian's postage charge was tiny and insulting. His living room held a paper kite from a previous holiday, now trembling in the open window's draft. He considered replacing the game with flying it. Rowan objected that a celebrated investor could not surrender anywhere this close to defeat. It would leave an unhelpful legacy. Mara offered to open the window wider.","“No bribe will save you.”","“What about a kiss?”","She took the kiss. The bank kept his ferry."],"questions":[],"wordCount":921,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0247\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵破产的三个小时</div><h1>The Duke Loses His Mortgage</h1><p id=\"chapter-0247-p1\">Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4961\" data-words=\"4961,4955\">lost</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8302\" data-words=\"8302\">tea</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6201\" data-words=\"6201\">plantation</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3209\" data-words=\"3209\">ferry</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3777\" data-words=\"3777\">half</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">his</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1244\" data-words=\"1244\">capital</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-892\" data-words=\"892\">before</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2364\" data-words=\"2364\">dinner</a>. The change would reinvent her island's economy, or so the promotional card claimed. Cassian wanted to take aim at its optimism with a pencil. Tavi reminded him that the model was deliberately limited. Its permissive planning rules existed to make play possible, not to settle actual policy.","The studio's first film did well at the box office. Gross revenue rose, but the profit margin remained modest after costs. Mara read the income statement before accepting another loan. Her bottom line mattered less than keeping the next payment manageable. She had grown fond of her imaginary employees. Their merit pay would remain.","Her next sales tax payment was due at the end of the round. Its effect on GDP was separate from the movement of money between players. Tavi had made the production counter explicit because earlier testers kept confusing output with wealth. The explanation could evoke an argument all by itself, though he was pleased anyone cared enough to ask.","Cassian had acquired a plantation again, this one growing tea on a different island. He needed capital for drainage. His mortgage rate had increased, and a tariff affected imported tools. The ferry's toll had become an expense he resented from the other side. Rowan enjoyed watching him discover that a charge could look different to its payer.","A subsidy card helped pay for repairs. The government treasury could fund only one allocation, however. Mara wanted a school; Rowan wanted a harbour. The exchequer's fiver did not expand when they argued. Tavi asked them to record expenditure before voting. The game would evoke a family quarrel if nobody kept its accounts straight.","The Gulf illustration had made Tavi nostalgic. He was knowledgeable about harbours and less certain about shops. A renowned designer had advised him to let different players criticize the model. He wanted them to specialize in finding what he missed. His legacy need not be a perfect system. An ingenious prompt to argue could be enough.","The exchequer needed cash, but another levy would hurt the small businesses. Its revenue from natural resources could help, provided it was not spent twice. Mara chose to spend time checking the table. An unreflecting answer would propel them into yet another dispute. She wanted to arrive at dinner before the next fictional recession.","A bad debt struck Rowan's island. His tenant had failed, leaving rental payments unpaid. He chose compensation for affected workers, then sold a small shareholding. His stake in the ferry declined. Cassian offered to buy it. Mara thought the price fair, but told both men to read the conditions first.","The game's bet square offered a jackpot. A winning throw could clear a mortgage; a losing one could spoil an entire round. Mara declined. Her savings account did not excite Cassian, but it had survived every crisis so far. The rules seemed to reward patience, a trait her husband possessed in almost every setting except games.","Cassian wanted to overcharge Rowan for the ferry's dividend. Rowan objected to the point of asking for a ruler and a lawyer. Tavi pointed at the printed amount. They could negotiate a sale, but not rewrite a payment they had already agreed.","He landed on detention and required bail. Rowan offered a loan on the exact terms Cassian had proposed earlier. Mara laughed until she had to put the ruby down. Her husband's earnest attempt to deny the symmetry failed. His premium cottage was now the only property he could sell quickly, though its book value exceeded what anyone would pay in haste.","The paper kite trembled over the mantel. Its tail brushed the game box, distracting Cassian from the next card. He made an unreflecting decision, then blamed the kite. Mara would not accept it as a scapegoat. His property might be a treasure, but its need for repairs was printed clearly enough for a player other than its owner to notice.","A postage charge complicated Mara's mail-order bookshop. Her studio still owed a royalty to the game's fictional novelist. Its coverage could not be omitted simply because the next production looked promising. A renowned player might recognize such traps. Mara was merely willing to read the card aloud. Tavi admired the difference.","The next storm required another insurance payment. The deductible was clearly printed. Cassian could not derive an exception from his expensive education. His legacy would be three broken umbrellas and an empty pocketbook, Rowan said. The debt-laden duke called the remark political. Mara asked whether that made it less funny.","Tavi's software engineering experience had helped him design the board, but he had left the computational process transparent. Prior to every major change, players could inspect the assumptions. They would not get a best-practice guide to economics; they would get an evening arguing about choices. He considered that a good cause for more tea.","Mara looked at Cassian's tiny island. Its real estate was in red ink, but its ferry still made a profit. She offered to buy him dinner with actual money. More than anything, she wanted the expression on his face preserved. He promised to remember that when she next needed help with a jar.","A generous cheque could not solve every problem. She had originally rejected an extra equity purchase because its premium price would leave her short. Its intangible promise was attractive, and it would spoil her budget. Previous</button><button data-go=\"248\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x051c03","title":"A Kiss Is Not Deductible","volume":"婚后外传：公爵破产的三个小时","anchor":"chapter-0249","paragraphs":["The board's final round began with Rowan attempting to sell a theatre he had forgotten he owned. Its box office had suffered. A royalty payment and a sales tax reduced its gross revenue to a much less pleasing profit margin. Tavi asked him to stop treating those figures as synonyms. Rowan had been doing so only to annoy Cassian, but agreed.","Rowan examined the compensation card and declared the promised amount inadequate. Tavi asked him to compare it with the deductible before arguing. The game did not reproduce every real contract, but its terms were plain. Its last installment would not disappear because the player was eloquent. He paid with the expression of a man betrayed by arithmetic.","Mara's income statement showed a steady business and a tolerable mortgage. Her savings account would cover the next storm's deductible. The bookshop's revenue had grown, its employees had received merit pay and its small ferry shareholding still paid a dividend. She was not the richest player in every category. She had simply survived the broadest collection of unlucky events.","Cassian's plantation was productive, his capital was exhausted and his bad debt had acquired a second page. The treasury offered a subsidy if he built a public road. A levy could cover part of the cost, but another toll would affect his own goods. The decision could not be solved by wishing the island more prosperous. His entire livelihood now seemed to depend on one sensible bridge.","Rowan called for a bet. Cassian declined. The jackpot had lost its attraction. He chose the road and retained a small stake in the ferry. Its dividend would arrive next round, after the game ended. The timing struck Mara as a particularly elegant form of punishment. His earnest expression did not help.","The final GDP figures were calculated. They measured the game's production total, not the kindness or wisdom of its players. The bottom line would have been too small a description of an evening in which everyone had laughed so hard. Tavi's legacy might be a successful game. Tonight it was a pile of crumbs and two powerful men arguing over miniature boats.","Mara held the ruby. “A ruby prize from royalty,” Rowan announced.","She offered Rowan a bribe in the form of washing-up help. He refused, then accepted a simple exchange of tasks. His island's expenditure had been vigorous enough to resemble a diplomatic crisis. A currency no bigger than a fingernail could nevertheless produce astonishing conviction. Tavi made a note to include fewer notes in the next version.","The final interest rate rise would entail a payment Cassian could not meet. His pension counter offered steady income, but no gamble could rescue him now. Mara bought his last premium boat. Its red ink did not spoil its charm. He would treasure its memory, he declared, and evoke its loss whenever she asked him to do anything other than rest. The unhelpful speech met laughter. A renowned negotiator had rarely been defeated by such small paper.","“Do not encourage her,” Cassian said.","“Her reign is already secure.”","Tavi packed the cards. The postage marker had gone missing beneath a cushion. Rowan found it beside the bail token and declared it evidence of a rescue conspiracy. Everyone knew he had put it there himself. His excuse became more elaborate the longer nobody believed it.","The tuition card had gone beneath the postage square. Mara rescued it. Tavi would spend time repairing the box tomorrow. The evening had given him a good cause to seek better storage, if nothing else. He could not be incapable of learning from his own game. A rubber band might solve what a celebrated consultant would otherwise charge to discuss.","Cassian began collecting plates. He had originally planned to win. Now he wanted to propel his wife towards the sofa and keep her there for the rest of the evening. She seemed to understand his new strategy, though she made him wait while she put the game away properly. He had never seen a victor so disciplined about cardboard.","He wanted yet another kiss, which seemed a reasonable use of defeat. Mara would not give it anywhere Rowan might make an unreflecting comment. She directed her husband towards the kitchen instead. He followed, accepting an ordinary task with less resistance than he had shown to the loss of an imaginary ferry.","The competition had threatened to reinvent their quiet dinner as a full economic summit. Tavi's permissive rules made that partly his fault. He promised a shorter version next time. Rowan would take aim at its unfinished details. Mara would spend time checking the cards. Cassian would bring fewer assumptions and perhaps another cake.","When their guests left, she found him holding the ruby counter. He placed it in her palm, then closed her fingers over it.","“An intangible asset,” he said.","“My legacy?”","“Your absolute authority over the next game.”","She laughed and drew him down beside her. The kiss was not deductible, not recorded in an income statement and not subject to the board's treasury. She offered it freely.
